6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement68C-7982D-0286D-0360D-0455D-0556D-0678
Group judgement

Risks

A684th of 49

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The 18-month forecasts are predictable in a way that is rare for a cyclical capital-goods company, and the predictability has a shape the memo should describe exactly. The earlier sets are congruent on the case and on the base (D-01), and the base through FY2027 rests on strengths ASML has demonstrated this year, not on ones it must acquire (D-02). The drivers are concentrated in three numbers (Low-NA units, the price reset, IBM upgrades) plus China policy, and two of the three are contracted for 2027, which is why the FY2027 cone is narrow (EPS 50 to 61 EUR around 56; revenue 52 to 59 billion EUR), mildly positive at its mode and fatter on the left than the right in its tails, with an upside capped by supply (D-03, D-04). Disclosure lets the Consumer verify FY2027 by April 2027 but not FY2028 before June (D-05); the transmission from plan to EPS is arithmetical in five of seven links, with the price percentage and the upgrade mix as the inferred two (D-06). The share price is about 62% sector-driven and the multiple sits at the bottom of its own range because earnings outran price, so the predictability of the P&L does not transfer to the share price in the window (D-07).

The risk that matters most inside 18 months is not a forecast miss; it is the optics of three dated events that will look like a peak while the fundamentals improve: a conservative January guide with a midpoint below consensus, the Q1 2027 seasonal step-down, and reported growth falling from about +50% to single digits by Q4 2027 on base effects. Behind them sit the real tails: a statutory China ban (about one in six inside the window, mostly in its last two quarters), a memory price break or AI-financing freeze that reaches upgrades first (about one in six), and a 2022-style supply slip (about one in eight). Beyond the window the same three drivers open up and the cone widens to -24% to +16% on FY2028 EPS, skewed down, with 2029 a decline rather than a pause at about 40% probability.

For the memo: carry the FY2027 base with heavy weight and a slight upward lean; carry FY2028 at 110 units with the June CMD as the resolving event; present riskiness (topic #9) as low on fundamentals and high on perception, with the sector's 62% share of daily variance as the number that joins the two; and treat the corrected cash flow (down payments included) as the line that warns first if the order book turns.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

A822nd of 49

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the perimeter is very narrow (one product family, two reporting lines, about five countries that matter), which makes the transmission from drivers to EPS highly predictable; what makes outcomes less predictable is concentration within that perimeter (four customers, two countries, memory's rising share), not breadth. The perimeter is shifting away from China toward Taiwan, Korea and the US.

  • Lines. Net system sales (EUV, DUV immersion and dry, metrology and inspection) and IBM; platforms NXE, EXE, NXT, XT, YieldStar, e-beam.
  • Countries. 2025 sales: China 29.1%, Taiwan 25.5%, Korea 25.0% (about 80% in three); Q3 2026 system sales: Taiwan 32%, Korea 31%, China 23%, US 13%.
  • Change. China from 40.5% of system revenue (2024) to about 20% of total revenue (2026); memory from minority to about half of system sales in 2026; US rising with onshored fabs.
  • Predictability. High on the arithmetic (capacity by platform times ASP, plus IBM, times GM, less a stable operating expense base); lower on timing (site acceptance, cleanroom readiness, annual-only bookings disclosure) and on policy (China licences).
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

A861st of 49

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: for the 18-month window the thesis rests almost entirely on strengths ASML has demonstrated and is demonstrating now: a 48% Low-NA unit increase being delivered in 2026 (49 systems recognised in nine months against 44 in all of 2025), price increases that have held through two downturns, an installed base that grew at 14% a year for a decade and at about 49% this year, a 61% drop-through of incremental revenue to EBIT, and a cash return policy that tracks cash. For FY2028 the thesis needs two strengths ASML has not yet shown: a third consecutive year of 30%-plus unit growth with the installation workforce to match, and gross-margin expansion during a capacity ramp (in 2017 and 2022 the ramp years saw gross margin fall). Those two are the acquired strengths, and they are also the two variables the market most wants to see at the June 2027 Capital Markets Day, which is why the 18-month forecasts are more predictable than the market's reaction to them.

Component by component: demonstrated, partly demonstrated, or to be acquired.

  • Low-NA output (65 in 2026, about 84 in 2027, 110 in 2028). 2026 is demonstrated: the Q3 count and the Q4 guide put 65 within reach, a 48% increase on 2025. 2027 is largely demonstrated by construction rather than execution: long-lead parts fix the number, Zeiss has committed to 85 and over 110 (Deutsche Bank 16 October 2026), and Morgan Stanley's cadence arithmetic (21 to 23 moves a quarter supporting a 90-unit run-rate by mid-2027) says the factory floor can do it. 2028 is to be acquired: ASML has never done three consecutive years of 30% unit growth (2019 to 2021 produced two, 26 to 31 to 42, and the third year, 2022, ended in a supply-driven guidance cut and fast shipments that deferred 2.8 billion EUR of revenue). The constraints named by Deutsche Bank and the own-3 report are module-build in Wilton and Taiwan, a sole-source Dutch mechatronics supplier for the wafer stage, large metal frames that cannot be machined quickly, VAT Group valve lead times stretching from three to nine months, and installation engineers that ASML competes for with its own customers. The T-D1 report's peer precedents (KLA's optics and laser shortages; Lam missing elevated expectations on mass-flow controllers and printed-circuit boards in October 2026) say that rapid ramps routinely trip on sub-tier suppliers. The second set's 110 is the prudent reading; 120 to 125 would be an acquired strength on top of an acquired strength.
  • Value-based pricing reaching gross margin. Demonstrated at the level of price: EUV ASPs have risen about 5% a year for years, GM held at 51.3% (company basis) through the 2023 to 2024 digestion, the NXE:3800E to 3800F step carries a price increase of 20 to 30% for 13% more throughput, and Korean parts prices rise 10% from January 2027. Not demonstrated at the level of flow-through during a ramp: management guided Q4 2026 GM down 50 basis points on higher revenue because capacity-ramp costs absorb part of the gain, and the T-D1 report shows GM fell to 41.8% in 2017 during the EUV ramp. The second set's assumption that about half the gross pricing benefit is absorbed is the right translation of a demonstrated strength into an undemonstrated setting.
  • IBM growing after a 50% year. Service growth with the fleet is demonstrated (14% compound since 2014; 95% of systems ever shipped still running; EUV service fees as tools leave warranty). Upgrades holding for one more year after a surge has one precedent (2021 upgrades pulled forward, yet 2022 service and field-option sales still rose 15.8%) and one counter-precedent (2023: upgrade guidance cut from +5% to flat when utilisation fell, with the Q4 2023 to Q1 2024 IBM drop from 1.6 to 1.3 billion EUR). The base's +16% in 2027 therefore rests on demonstrated behaviour in a capacity-tight year; the +6% in 2028 rests on an assumption no cycle has tested at this scale.
  • High-NA margin maturation. The Low-NA precedent (zero GM on the first NXE:3300 systems in 2014, corporate average about five years later) makes the second set's path (20 to 25% GM in 2026 to 2027, about 35% in 2028, about 45% by 2029 to 2030) a demonstrated pattern applied again; fleet availability near 90% ahead of Low-NA at the same stage says the learning curve is faster this time.
  • Operating leverage. Demonstrated this year (61% drop-through) and in the flat years (2022 and 2024: flat revenue, flat EBIT). The base's 57% for 2027 and 2028 is inside the record.
  • Capital return. Demonstrated both ways: 5.8 billion EUR of buybacks in 2025 and 6.0 billion in nine months of 2026 when cash arrived; a pause from April 2023 to late 2024 when inventory trapped cash. The base's sizing to cash is what ASML does.
  • Re-allocation under export controls. Demonstrated once at small scale (late-2023 licence revocation on NXT:2050i and 2100i backfilled; change orders to NXT:1980i). Not demonstrated at the scale of a whole-country immersion ban.

Guidance record as evidence of delivery capacity. Two cuts in four years (July 2022, supply; October 2024, customer push-outs) and three raises in 2026. The comparable environment for 2027 to 2028 is 2021 to 2022: demand was beaten, supply forced the cut. That precedent is exactly why the acquired strength (2028 output) deserves the moderate weight the second set gave it, and why FY2027, where the supply number is fixed, deserves heavy weight.

Peer read. ASM International reached its 2030 target three years early on a content cycle without a comparable manufacturing ramp; BESI's valuation fell 54% when one adoption date slipped despite margins holding; Applied Materials and Lam show that service businesses cushion downturns at operating margins of 28 to 33%. None has attempted a 30%-plus unit ramp for three years with a sole-source optics chain; the nearest precedent is ASML itself in 2019 to 2022, which is the cautionary one.

For the memo. The expected change through FY2027 is plausible on demonstrated strengths; the FY2028 step is plausible on strengths being built in plain sight (Zeiss's 25,000 square metres at Oberkochen, VAT's Malaysia plant, 1,000 Taiwan hires, Brainport) but not yet shown; the memo should present 2028 as the year in which ASML must prove something new, and 2027 as the year in which it must only continue.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?B6011th of 49full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

C+5512th of 49

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: over the 18-month window the cone is narrow for a semiconductor-equipment company and narrow in absolute terms for revenue, ordinary for margin and wide only at its policy edge. My 80% range for FY2027 EPS is 50 to 61 EUR around a base of 56 (consensus 57.55), that is about -11% to +9%; for FY2027 revenue 52 to 59 billion EUR around 56.0. The centre of the cone leans slightly up (upgrades and gross margin have beaten in every quarter of 2026; the first-year price effect has historically surprised on margin; the Q1 seasonal input is conservative) while the left tail is fatter than the right (a statutory China ban, a memory price break pulling upgrades, a supply slip of the 2022 kind), so the distribution is best described as a mildly positive mode with a negative skew in the tails, and an upside hard-capped by supply. Predictability is therefore high for the P&L through Q4 2027, moderate for Q1 2028 (the first quarter that depends on 2028 units), and low for the share price, which the sector drives (D-07).

Building the cone from the drivers (my arithmetic on the base; each range is an 80% band, independent unless stated).

  • Low-NA units: 82 to 87 in FY2027 (capacity about 85, fixed; the variable is site acceptance and cleanroom readiness; Morgan Stanley reports a DRAM customer already pushing tools from December 2026 to March 2027). ±2.5 units at about 254 million EUR and above-group margin: about ±1.3 billion EUR revenue, ±1.5 EUR of EPS.
  • IBM: 12.7 to 15.7 billion EUR against 14.2. Service is predictable (fleet times fee); upgrades are not (6.4 billion EUR assumed, near the 2026 run-rate). ±1.5 billion EUR, almost all upgrades at about 90% margin: ±2.8 EUR of EPS. This is the largest single source of FY2027 variance and the one with the best early warning (the January IBM guide; the Q1 2027 print against about 3.3 billion EUR).
  • China DUV: ±2 billion EUR around the base's roughly 8.4 billion EUR of China revenue (about 15% of the year), at about group margin: ±2 EUR of EPS, excluding a statutory ban.
  • Gross-margin translation: ±1 point around 57.6% (pricing flow-through against ramp and High-NA costs): ±1.2 EUR.
  • Pricing timing: the CFO's "partial" 2027 benefit could be front-loaded (new orders at new prices shipping from mid-2027): +1 to +2 EUR, no symmetric downside because the increases are signed.
  • Operating expenses, D&A, tax, net financial items: ±0.7 EUR combined; the share count is sized to cash and is a small positive. Combined as independent variables the 80% band is about ±5.5 EUR, i.e. 50.5 to 61.5; I round to 50 to 61. The probability that FY2027 EPS lands at or above the base is about 60%, and at or above consensus (57.55) about 45%.

The tails, with probabilities for the 18-month window.

  • A MATCH-type statutory ban taking effect inside the window (about one in six): FY2027 EPS about 52 EUR on the second set's sizing (-8%), lower (about 48 to 50) if the ban is enacted in December 2026 and implemented quickly, because re-allocation takes seven to twelve months and receiving cleanrooms must exist. Q1 2028 would carry more of it.
  • A memory price break or AI-financing freeze in 2027 (about one in six for an effect inside the window): upgrades fall first (customers stop buying throughput when utilisation drops: the 2023 precedent), then DUV volume, then 2028 units; FY2027 EPS about 53 EUR, Q1 2028 well below the base's 15.4 billion EUR of revenue.
  • A supply slip of the 2022 kind (about one in eight): revenue deferred by fast shipments rather than lost; FY2027 EPS -3 to -5 EUR, recovered in 2028.
  • The upside tail (about one in eight): upgrades hold at 7 billion EUR, pricing fully visible by H2, GM 59%: FY2027 EPS about 61 to 63 EUR, which is the ceiling because 2027 units cannot rise. The tails are correlated (a financing freeze lowers the political cost of a China ban only in the sense that both hit the same year), and their joint probability is small but not negligible; that is what makes the skew negative below the mode.

The quarterly shape inside the window, which is where the market will misread predictability. The base's quarterly revenue runs 14.8 (Q4 2026), 12.6, 13.4, 14.0, 16.0 (Q1 to Q4 2027) and 15.4 billion EUR (Q1 2028): year-on-year +52%, +44%, +44%, +20%, +8%, +22%. Two features deserve the memo's attention. First, the Q4-to-Q1 decline: the base assumes -15%; the 2025 and 2026 precedents were -16% and -10% (the T-D2 and own-5 reports both document the about -11% guided into Q1 2026), so the Q1 2027 print is more likely to beat the base than to miss it. Second, the reported growth rate falls to +20% in Q3 2027 and +8% in Q4 2027 purely on the 2026 comparison base, while the sequential run-rate keeps rising. The own-5 report's precedents say the market de-rates on decelerating growth even when it is a base effect (ASML's multiple fell 34% in 2017 to 2018 with 21 points of the fall before any inventory correction; FY2019 estimates flattened from Q4 2017), and that the stock recovers when the deceleration proves to be optical (July 2022: ASML outperformed despite out-quarter cuts of 25 to 35%). The P&L is predictable; the narrative around it is not.

The width beyond the window, for the three-year view. FY2028 EPS 57 to 87 EUR around 75 (-24% to +16%), skewed down because the downside combines units, upgrades and policy while the upside is capped at about 120 units; FY2029 60 to 95 EUR with a 40% probability (my figure; the second set used one in three) that the year is a decline rather than a pause. The T-D2 report's historical dispersion (2001 and 2009: 20 points of gross-margin compression in three quarters; three to four years of mediocre returns) belongs to this horizon, not to the next 18 months, because the 2027 deliveries are prepaid.

Peer calibration. ASM International guided FY2027 above 5.7 billion EUR on 27 October 2026 and Jefferies judged the room above it "limited"; BESI's cone was one adoption date; VAT Group's is its order book, which just beat consensus by 35% with lead times stretched to nine months. Among these, ASML's 18-month cone is the narrowest on revenue because it is the only one whose output is fixed by its own supply chain a year ahead and paid for in advance.

Predictability, stated plainly. The probability that FY2027 revenue lands within 5% of the base is about 70%, that EPS lands within 10% about 75%, and that the 18-month column's earnings (which the model's TSR uses) land within 10% about 70%. For a cyclical capital-goods company these are unusually high; the exposure is the last quarter of the window and the policy tail.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

C+5614th of 49

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: disclosure is helpful on the lines that decide FY2027 and unhelpful on the lines that decide FY2028, and the next six months contain the two events that will add most to it. ASML discloses quarterly: revenue and gross margin with a one-quarter guide, system sales by technology (EUV, immersion, dry, KrF, i-line, metrology) and by region, the number of EUV systems recognised, IBM revenue with a quarterly guide, R&D and SG&A, and current and non-current contract liabilities. It does not disclose quarterly bookings (annual backlog only, next on 27 January 2027), the split of IBM between service and upgrades, the price percentage of the value-based reset, the 2028 unit plan, High-NA system count or margin, or the share of China revenue that is servicing. The monitoring plan below can verify the base case's FY2027 revenue to within about 3% by April 2027 and its margin to within about half a point; it cannot verify the 2028 units or the upgrade normalisation until June 2027.

What the next six months will add, dated.

  • 10 November 2026: the US Affiliates Rule suspension and China's rare-earth suspension expire unless extended. Binary; the read is whether the truce holds.
  • December 2026: the year-end defence bill (the MATCH Act's vehicle). The read is enactment and implementation language (150-day allied alignment window; Morgan Stanley expects a year or more to implement).
  • 27 January 2027 (Q4 2026 results): FY2026 out-turn (base 44.6 billion EUR; GM about 56%); the FY2027 guide (base-consistent range about 52 to 58 billion EUR, GM 56 to 58%); the Q1 2027 guide (base about 12.6 billion EUR; a guide above 13.0 billion would confirm the upward lean); the FY2027 IBM guide (at least 13.5 billion EUR supports the base's 14.2); the annual backlog (the first company figure since 38.8 billion EUR; Bernstein infers about 56; above about 45 supports 2028 at 110 units); the 2027 capacity confirmation (85 Low-NA, 170 immersion) and any 2028 number; 2027 capex; the buyback (12 billion EUR programme half used); the FY2026 tax rate (about 17%).
  • April 2027 (Q1 2027 results): IBM at or above 3.3 billion EUR (the early-turn threshold); China share of system sales at or below 20%; EUV systems recognised (a run-rate of 20 or more supports 84 for the year); Q2 guide; non-current CL (a quarter-on-quarter fall would be the first cash warning).
  • Also inside six months: TSMC's January 2027 capex guide (Jefferies expects 80 to 85 billion US dollars; a cut of more than 10% is the alarm); Samsung, SK hynix and Micron 2027 capex; Lam and Applied results as WFE read-across; monthly China lithography import data (Bernstein's regression explains about 95% of ASML's China system sales); VAT Group orders as a sub-tier signal; Korean parts-price confirmation. The 10 June 2027 Capital Markets Day falls just outside six months and is the event that resolves 2028 units, the price percentage, the margin path and a new 2030 framework.

How each thesis driver can be tracked, and how well.

  • Low-NA units (FY2027): well. EUV systems recognised are disclosed quarterly; the capacity number is public; Taiwan and Korea import data (Bernstein's trackers) give a second view. Verification by April 2027: good.
  • Pricing: poorly in level, adequately in effect. No percentage will be disclosed; the GM guide for FY2027 and the Q2 2027 GM are the observables. The second set's rule stands: a FY2027 GM guide at or below the Q4 2026 exit rate (about 57%) despite pricing means capacity cost absorbs more than half the benefit.
  • IBM upgrades: partly. IBM is guided quarterly and reported, but the service and upgrade split is not disclosed; a sequential IBM fall of more than 20% in Q1 2027 or a flat FY2027 IBM guide would signal the turn. The own-4 report notes the share of upgrades is not disclosed even annually; expert calls put logic customers at 25 to 30% of lithography capital on upgrades and memory at about 40%.
  • China: adequately. Region shares of system sales quarterly; import data monthly; but the servicing share and the designated-entity share are not disclosed (own-2).
  • Orders: indirectly but reliably. Non-current CL (up 5 to 6 billion EUR in Q3 2026) is the proxy; the own-3 report shows CL turned four to six quarters ahead of revenue in past cycles and that fast shipments can distort the current portion, so non-current CL is the line to watch.
  • 2028 units, High-NA count and margin, the retention award's exact size, the innovation-box renewal: not trackable before June 2027 or later.

Where disclosure is worse than peers and why it matters. Applied, Lam and KLA give backlog or bookings colour quarterly; Tokyo Electron gives none but has four- to five-month lead times that make orders less informative. ASML withdrew quarterly bookings at the peak of order momentum, which the first set flagged; the practical consequence for the next six months is that the January backlog figure will carry more weight in one print than any quarterly figure used to, and the market's dispersion on FY2028 will not narrow until then. The second consequence is that the fast-shipment and site-acceptance mechanics (revenue recognised on customer acceptance; the own-5 report's 700 million EUR Q1 2020 deferral) can move a quarter by 3 to 5% without any change in demand, so single-quarter revenue misses should be read against CL and the unit count, not alone.

Net. For the lines that set the 18-month forecast, trackability is above the sector's average (unit count, IBM guide, region mix, CL); for the lines that set the memo's months 18 to 36 it is below (no bookings, no 2028 plan, no price size, no upgrade split). The six months ahead contain the two policy dates on the negative side and the January print on the positive side, and the January print is the single most informative disclosure in the whole window.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

A781st of 49

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the internal transmission is unusually clear, because the company states its plan in physical units (systems a year by platform), prices are visible in the product cadence, the two reported revenue lines map directly onto those units and onto the fleet, and the cost base is small and stable; of the seven links from "what ASML says" to EPS, five are disclosed and two (the price percentage and the upgrade share of IBM) must be inferred. The external transmission is also clear in its mechanics but long in its lags: AI capex becomes ASML revenue twelve to twenty-four months later through customers' capex, fab construction and prepayments, and policy can short-circuit the chain by decree. The lags are the predictability for 18 months and the uncertainty beyond.

The internal chain, link by link, with the observable for each.

  1. Stated plan → units. Management states capacity by platform and year (Low-NA about 65, 85, more than 110; immersion about 130, 170, up to 220; four to five High-NA recognitions in 2026) and reports EUV systems recognised quarterly. Clear; the 2028 number is the missing term.
  2. Units × price → EUV and non-EUV system revenue. The blended Low-NA ASP is inferable (about 235 million EUR in 2026 from EUV revenue over units; the 3800E at about 220 and 3800F at about 290 million EUR per Bernstein; EXE:5200B about 360 million EUR). The value-based reset's size is undisclosed; its direction and timing (partial 2027, full 2028) are stated. So: units clear, price direction clear, price size inferred. The base carries +8% a year in blended Low-NA ASP for 2027 and 2028.
  3. Fleet × fee + upgrades → IBM. Service is fleet-driven (about 5% of EUV ASP a year; lifetime service up to 130% of tool value); upgrades are discretionary and undisclosed within IBM. Guided quarterly. Clear in total, opaque in composition; the composition is what moves gross margin.
  4. Mix + price − ramp costs → gross margin. Software upgrades near 100% GM, Low-NA above group, High-NA 20 to 25% early, DUV near group: the bridge is stated qualitatively by management (upgrades as the "swing factor"; ramp costs, High-NA mix and 3800F reliance as offsets) and quantified by brokers (Jefferies' 400 basis points; Morgan Stanley's 20% High-NA margin). The base's 55.7% to 57.6% to 58.3% path is a disclosed-direction, inferred-size link.
  5. Gross profit − operating expenses → EBIT. R&D and SG&A are guided quarterly (about 1.2 and 0.4 billion EUR); the delayering saving (about 100 to 200 million EUR from May 2027) and the retention award (about 300 million EUR a year, 2027 to 2029, straight-line per the own-6 report) net to roughly nothing. Clear. The drop-through is the cleanest arithmetic in the case: a 10% change in revenue moves EBIT by 12 to 14% at a 45% margin; a 5% miss concentrated in upgrades costs about 9% of EBIT.
  6. EBIT + net financial items − tax → net income. Net financial items about 0.5 to 0.75 billion EUR a year (interest on net cash; the Zeiss SMT equity result); the Mistral revaluation (509.7 million EUR, tax-exempt under the participation exemption, confirmed by the Q3 accounts) is the one-off; tax about 17% guided for 2026 and about 17.5% while the innovation-box ruling runs to 2028, with Pillar Two immaterial (0.1 million EUR in H1 2026). Each point of tax is about 1.2% of EPS. Clear.
  7. Net income ÷ shares → EPS. Buybacks are reported quarterly and sized to cash; the share count falls about 1.7% (2027) and 2.3% (2028) in the base; the retention award settles from treasury shares (about 0.6 million shares in 2030). Clear. In sum: links 1, 5, 6 and 7 are disclosed and arithmetical; links 2 and 4 are directionally disclosed and sized by inference; link 3 is disclosed in total and opaque in mix. The milestones that verify the chain are the ones listed in D-05 (unit count, IBM guide, GM guide, backlog, CL).

The external chain, and where it is clear or lagged. AI demand → hyperscaler and neocloud capex (now visibly financing-dependent: hyperscaler FCF near zero in 2026 to 2027, neocloud spreads wide) → foundry and memory capex (TSMC 60 to 64 billion US dollars in 2026, about 80 to 85 in 2027; Micron above 50 billion in fiscal 2027; SK hynix in the high 40 trillion KRW) → fab construction (about 3.5 years to full operation; cleanroom preparation 15 to 18 months; tool move-in six to eight months) → tool orders with 35 to 40% down payments (non-current CL) → shipment (lead times 12 months or more for EUV) → site acceptance → revenue. Each link is observable with public data at a lag, which is why a demand shock reaches ASML's P&L a year or more after it reaches the hyperscalers' credit spreads, and why the first six months of 2027 are protected whatever the credit market does. The parallel chain, lithography intensity (DRAM EUV layers from three at 1b to five or six at 1c and eight at 1d; logic roughly flat at N2, rising at A14 and A10), converts the same wafer capacity into more exposures and is structural. The policy chain is short: a statutory ban → immersion China (23% of Q3 2026 system sales, guided to about 20% of 2026 revenue) → re-allocation over seven to twelve months; servicing China (Morgan Stanley: up to 10 to 15% of IBM; an expert put China at about 3% of global service revenue, which I treat as too low) → no re-allocation. The memo should present the external chain as clear and slow, with policy as the one fast link.

The EPS sensitivities the memo should carry (my arithmetic on the base). One Low-NA system: about 0.5 EUR of FY2027 EPS. One billion EUR of upgrades: about 1.9 EUR. One point of gross margin: about 1.2 EUR. One billion EUR of China DUV revenue: about 1.0 EUR. One point of tax: about 0.7 EUR. One billion EUR of buybacks at 1,400 EUR a share: about 0.19% of the count, about 0.1 EUR a year. A statutory China ban in a full year: about -4.5 to -5.5 EUR.

Where the chain is least clear, and what it costs. The upgrade share of IBM and the price percentage are the two unknowns that decide whether FY2028 gross margin is 57% or 59.5%, which is about 3 EUR of EPS; and the 2028 unit number decides about 13 EUR of FY2028 EPS between 95 and 120 units. Those three numbers are the whole of the FY2028 dispersion, and none is in the chain the company discloses today.

Judgement: perception risk is high and partly already realised: the AI-chain financing scare, the sector's "de-rate before the peak" playbook and crowded positioning can each move the shares 10 to 20% regardless of ASML's own news, and the regime (risk-off, Momentum Underweight, Semiconductors Underweight) amplifies them.

  • AI financing read-across. Neocloud and hyperscaler credit spreads, CoreWeave's bonds, Oracle and SoftBank sell-offs, token-price deflation, hyperscaler capex commentary: in October 2026 ASML's technical momentum fell from the strongest reading to a break within a week while its fundamentals improved.
  • Cycle-peak narrative. Mitigants: 98% free float, about 2 billion US dollars of daily traded value, short interest about 0.15%.
  • Macro. US 10-year near 5.10%; a hawkish December Fed; energy-led inflation (Brent 111 US dollars) all act through the discount rate.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

B729th of 49

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the company follows a "conservative start, staged raises" pattern: FY2026 revenue guidance was raised three times (about +22% at the midpoint from January to October) and GM guidance twice; the chief executive pairs measured formal guidance with expansive qualitative commentary. Before 2026 the record included two externally driven cuts, so the upgrading behaviour is reliable in an up-cycle and less so at turning points.

  • 2026: January 34 to 39 billion EUR at 51 to 53% GM; April 36 to 40 billion; July 43 to 45 billion at 54 to 56%; October 44 to 45 billion at about 56%.
  • Quarterly: revenue above the guided midpoint in each of the four quarters to Q1 2026 (by 2.3 to 2.9%), above consensus in Q2 2026 (5.4%), in line in Q3 with margin well above.
  • Earlier: July 2022 FY2022 growth cut from 20% to 10% (supply); October 2024 FY2025 range cut and GM midpoint cut by 300 basis points (customer push-outs; shares -16%).
  • Nature: the company upgrades on evidence (orders, down payments) rather than in anticipation; mid-term targets are left unchanged through cycles and reset only at investor days.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C+6412th of 49

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: over one year the record is consistent beating (revenue at or above guidance and consensus, margins well above); over three and five years it includes two material guidance cuts, both at points where external conditions shifted (supply in 2022, customer push-outs in 2024). In the comparable environment (a supply-constrained up-cycle, 2021 to 2022), demand was beaten but supply forced a cut, which is the relevant warning for the 2028 capacity plan.

  • Last year. Revenue above the guided midpoint in Q2 2025 to Q1 2026 (by 2.3 to 2.9%); Q2 2026 revenue 5.4% above consensus, GM 54.0% against 51 to 52% guided, EPS 11% above consensus; Q3 2026 revenue in line (11.66 billion against about 11.70 billion EUR), GM 57.5% against 55 to 57% guided and about 56.1% consensus, underlying EPS about 9% above (reported 21%); Q4 guide above consensus; FY2026 raised three times. Note that Q3's beat came from IBM (about 0.4 billion EUR above guide) while system sales were about 5% below consensus (CCB International).
  • Three years. FY2024 midpoint lowered on customer push-outs; October 2024 FY2025 range and GM cut (shares -16%); FY2025 delivered at 32.7 billion EUR (+15.6%), above the cut range's midpoint.
  • Five years. July 2022 FY2022 growth cut to 10% from 20% on supply; 2023 China headwind of 10 to 15% factored after trilateral controls; FY2021 and FY2023 strong out-turns.
  • Pattern. Beats in up-cycles driven by mix and margin; misses at turning points or when supply binds.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

B+624th of 49

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the forecasts should carry heavy weight for FY2026 and FY2027, moderate weight for FY2028 and low weight for FY2029 and FY2030. The track record says to lean FY2027 slightly upward (ASML beats in supply-constrained up-cycles, on margin and installed base rather than units), FY2028 units slightly downward (the one comparable environment, 2021 to 2022, produced a supply-driven guidance cut), and FY2029 clearly downward (growth years have always been followed by digestion). The single most useful near-term implication: ASML's habit of a conservative opening guide means the 27 January 2027 FY2027 range is more likely than not to have a midpoint below today's consensus (57.5 billion EUR), even though the year is more likely than not to end at or above my base (56.0 billion EUR).

The record, by horizon.

  • Quarter. Revenue above the guided midpoint by 2.3 to 2.9% in each of the four quarters to Q1 2026; Q2 2026 5.4% above consensus; Q3 2026 in line on revenue (11,662 against about 11,700 million EUR) with GM 140-plus basis points above consensus and underlying EPS about 9% above. The beats come from IBM and margin; Q3 system sales were about 5% below consensus (CCB International). Lesson: weight quarterly revenue forecasts heavily (error about ±3%); weight quarterly GM forecasts with an upward lean of about 0.5 to 1 point while upgrades are strong.
  • Year. FY2026: opening guide 34 to 39 billion EUR (midpoint +12%) raised three times to 44 to 45 billion (about +36%). FY2025: the October 2024 cut (range 30 to 35 billion, GM midpoint -300 basis points) on customer push-outs, then delivery of 32.7 billion, above the cut range's midpoint. FY2022: growth cut from 20% to 10% in July 2022 on supply. Lesson: in an up-cycle with orders in hand the out-turn exceeds the opening guide by a wide margin; at turning points or when supply binds, the guide is cut.
  • Mid-term. The 2030 framework (44 to 60 billion EUR, GM 56 to 60%) set in November 2024 is already at its floor in FY2026; my FY2028 (69.7 billion) and FY2030 (78.8 billion) sit 16% and 31% above its ceiling. The framework is not a forecast and should carry no weight; the June 2027 Capital Markets Day will replace it.
  • Consensus record. FY2026 EPS consensus rose 59% in twelve months, FY2027 79%, FY2028 106%: consensus has been far too low throughout this up-cycle, and it was too high in 2024. Revisions follow company guidance with a lag; they do not anticipate it.
  • Our own record. The prior memo (23 September 2026) carried FY2026 EPS of 39.0 EUR and FY2027 of 51.5 EUR; one month later FY2026 is about 42.7 EUR (+9.5%), and my FY2027 is 56.1 EUR (+9%). Our house forecasts have also been too low, because they extrapolated a plateau the order book did not support. I have not inherited the plateau: units in my base are capacity-paced through 2028.

How much weight, quantified (my judgement of the 80% range).

  • FY2026: EPS 41.8 to 43.5 EUR; revenue 44.2 to 45.2 billion. The Q4 guide (14.2 to 15.2 billion) bounds it. Near-certain.
  • FY2027: EPS 50 to 61 EUR around my 56.1; revenue 52 to 59 billion. Units are fixed by long-lead parts (about 85 Low-NA), so the error is in IBM (±1.5 billion EUR), China DUV (±2 billion) and GM (±1 point). Heavy weight.
  • FY2028: EPS 57 to 87 EUR around 74.9; revenue 60 to 77 billion. Units (95 to 120 Low-NA), the full pricing effect, upgrade normalisation and the MATCH Act all bite. Moderate weight.
  • FY2029 and FY2030: EPS 60 to 95 EUR and 65 to 110 EUR. The base rate of digestion and the AI-financing loop dominate. Low weight; carry scenarios, not a point.

Direction of lean, and why.

  • FY2027 up, modestly (about +2 to +4% on EPS against my base). Three track-record patterns point up: upgrades have beaten guidance (Q3 by about 400 million EUR; Q4 guide 23% above consensus) and management says more upgrade products are coming; value-based pricing is signed; and the first-year price effect has historically surprised on margin, not revenue. My base already puts revenue below consensus on China; the lean therefore narrows rather than reverses that gap.
  • FY2028 down on units, neutral on margin. The comparable environment (2021 to 2022) is a supply-constrained ramp that ended in a supply-driven cut. Management has refused to give a 2028 unit number while saying 30% is not enough; module-build (Wilton, Taiwan), specialist suppliers and installation staff are the named constraints. I hold 110 units, not more. On margin, the record of beating supports my 58.3%, but upgrade normalisation is new territory and offsets the lean.
  • FY2029 down. Every multi-year growth run at ASML has been followed by a digestion year (FY2021 +33% then +14%; FY2023 +30% then +2.6%; FY2022 EPS -1.5% and FY2024 -3.3%). A three-year run of 24 to 36% growth (2026 to 2028) would be the longest in its history. My base +2.8% is already a slowdown; the lean is toward a decline.

The January 2027 trap for set four. If ASML guides FY2027 as it guided FY2026 (an opening range with a midpoint well below its own internal plan), a range of roughly 52 to 58 billion EUR with GM of 56 to 58% is consistent with both its habit and my base. That midpoint (about 55 billion) sits about 4% below today's consensus revenue; the market could read a conservative guide as a peak signal even though it is the same behaviour that preceded a 22% raise in 2026. The Consumer should weigh the guide's floor, the annual backlog and the IBM guide, not the midpoint.

Financial forecasts

A871st of 49

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts describe the strongest earnings phase in ASML's history, and its shape is more predictable than its length. Through FY2028 the arithmetic is unusually traceable: units capped by capacity and largely ordered, prices signed, operating expenses growing at half the rate of revenue, no leverage, a neutral tax and interest line, and buybacks sized to cash. That arithmetic gives revenue of about 70 billion EUR, an EBIT margin of about 47% and EPS of about 75 EUR in FY2028, close to consensus. The forecast is less certain in three places that the third and fourth sets should test hardest: the size of the 2028 unit step (supply, not demand, is the limit), the speed at which IBM upgrades normalise as customers' cleanrooms open (the margin mix), and China under a possible statutory ban (milder than feared while capacity is short, harsher later). Beyond 2028 the reinforcing loops turn in a known order (upgrades, then down payments, then units), so FY2029 is a pause in my base and a decline in a plausible downside, and the cash line will show it before the P&L. For the 18-month window the cone is narrow and mildly skewed up on margin; the risk to the share price in that window is less a miss than the optics of a conservative January guide and of reported growth falling from about 50% to single digits by Q4 2027 on comparison base alone.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

C+6013th of 49

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: below operating profit, the next four years are roughly neutral for net income and positive for EPS, the reverse of FY2019 to FY2025, when a rising tax rate took about 12% off EPS. The ratio of net income to EBIT drifts only from 0.85 (FY2025) to 0.84 (FY2028) and 0.84 (FY2030), so net income grows almost exactly with EBIT; buybacks then add about 2 percentage points a year to EPS growth. The two items that matter are the tax rate after the Dutch innovation-box ruling expires (end-2028) and the size of the buyback; the one-off to strip is the 509.7 million EUR Mistral gain in FY2026. Free cash flow is where below-the-line items matter most: contract-liability swings move FCF by several billion EUR a year, so FCF will grow less smoothly than EPS and will fall in FY2029 in my base while EPS still rises.

Item by item (my base; history in brackets).

  • Net financial items (including the Zeiss SMT equity result). +1,100 million EUR in FY2026 (509.7 million Mistral, about 590 million interest and associates), then +550, +600, +650, +750 million as net cash rises from about 13 to about 27 billion EUR [FY2025 +105 million; the pre-tax line has exceeded EBIT in every year since FY2021 except FY2022]. About 2% of pre-tax profit; it neither drives nor threatens EPS.
  • Mistral AI. An 11% stake (1.3 billion EUR invested, September 2025), carried at fair value by a venture-capital method; the September 2026 round led by Samsung valued Mistral above 21 billion EUR and produced the Q3 gain. It is non-operating and can reverse. I include it in FY2026 reported EPS (42.7 EUR) and show underlying EPS of 41.4 EUR; the FactSet median (42.49) appears to include it and the mean (41.19) appears not to, so the Consumer should compare like with like. I model no further revaluations. My inference (not confirmed in the sources) is that the gain is exempt from Dutch tax under the participation exemption, which is consistent with the Q3 tax rate of 16.1% against 17.5% in Q2.
  • Tax rate. 16.6% (FY2026, lowered by the exempt gain), 17.5% (FY2027 and FY2028), 18.0% (FY2029 and FY2030) [6.9% FY2019, 13.7%, 15.2%, 15.0%, 15.8%, 18.6%, 17.7% FY2025]. The innovation box (9% on qualifying income) is renewed for 2024 to 2028; Pillar Two (in force in the Netherlands from January 2024) sets a 15% floor that ASML's blended rate already exceeds. I raise the rate to 18% from 2029 to reflect renewal risk on less favourable terms. Sensitivity: each point of tax is about 1.2% of EPS; the prior memo's 21% stress costs about 4%.
  • Minorities and other investments. None material. Zeiss SMT (24.9%) is equity-accounted; Zeiss loans (about 1.9 billion EUR at 0.01 to 1% interest) carry a hidden opportunity cost of perhaps 50 to 70 million EUR a year, which is inside net financial items.
  • One-offs. Severance for the delayering (perhaps 50 to 100 million EUR in 2027) and the retention award (about 300 million EUR a year of share-based pay, 2027 to 2029) are in operating expenses, not below the line; brokers are unlikely to adjust either out, so they are on the consensus basis.
  • Share count. Weighted average basic shares 383.6 million (FY2026), 377.0 (FY2027), 368.8 (FY2028), 361.2 (FY2029), 353.4 (FY2030) [419.8 million FY2019 to 385.4 million FY2025, about -1.4% a year]. Buybacks of 7.8, 13, 17, 13 and 21 billion EUR at assumed average prices of 1,400 to 2,100 EUR; employee-plan issuance of about 0.3 to 0.4 million shares a year and about 1 million in FY2030 (including the retention award). The share price assumption matters: at prices 20% higher the FY2028 count is about 1.2% higher.
  • Dividend. 8.80 EUR (FY2026; three interims of 1.88 EUR and a final of about 3.16), 10.80, 13.20, 14.50, 16.50 EUR: a payout of 19 to 21%, against 30 to 45% in FY2019 to FY2025 [7.50 EUR in FY2025]. ASML is shifting returns toward buybacks, which consensus (DPS 8.62, 10.21, 13.13) also assumes.

EPS reconciliation, FY2025 to FY2028 (my base). EPS rises from 24.73 to 74.9 EUR, a factor of 3.03: revenue x2.13, EBIT margin (34.6% to 47.2%) x1.36, net income to EBIT (0.850 to 0.840) x0.99, share count (388.6 to 368.8 million weighted) x1.05. Revenue does about two-thirds of the work in logarithmic terms, margin about 28%, the share count about 5%, and below-EBIT items subtract about 1%. Over FY2019 to FY2025, by contrast, the tax rate alone subtracted about 12% and the share count added about 9%.

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: my base case is a capacity-paced, price-assisted up-cycle that runs through FY2028 and pauses in FY2029: revenue 44.6, 56.0, 69.7, 71.6 and 78.8 billion EUR (FY2026 to FY2030), EBIT margin rising from 41.7% to about 47% and holding, EPS 42.7, 56.1, 74.9, 77.4 and 89.7 EUR, and free cash flow 15.0, 20.3, 23.6, 19.9 and 32.9 billion EUR. It is close to consensus for FY2026 to FY2028 on EPS and margin, below it on revenue (China and High-NA), and clearly below it for FY2029. The case rests on four drivers, in order of importance: Low-NA units (capacity), the size and timing of value-based pricing, the path of IBM upgrades as customers' cleanrooms open, and China DUV under export controls. The first is the most predictable to 2027 and the least predictable after 2028.

The four drivers, each with its reasoning, its evidence and the observable that would move it.

  1. Low-NA units. 2027 is fixed near 85 by long-lead parts and is close to fully ordered; I recognise 84. 2028 at 110 is the company's earlier plan; management says 30% is insufficient and is investigating more, but the constraints (module-build in Wilton and Taiwan, specialist suppliers, installation staff) are physical, and the 2022 precedent argues against assuming the buy side's 120 to 125. 2029 at 112 assumes orders hold at 2028 levels without growth. Observable: the 2028 unit number at the January 2027 results or the June 2027 CMD; Zeiss and module-build milestones.
  2. Pricing. Like-for-like +3 to 4% a year on systems in 2027 and 2028 plus new-model mix (3800F); about +2.5 points of GM gross by 2028, about half absorbed. Observable: the FY2027 GM guide (my base implies 57 to 58%); any disclosed price percentage at the CMD; whether parts price rises spread beyond Korea.
  3. IBM upgrades. Held near the 2026 run-rate in 2027 (6.4 billion EUR), then falling as cleanrooms open; service grows 12 to 15% a year with the fleet. Observable: the FY2027 IBM guide in January; a Q1 2027 IBM print below about 3.3 billion EUR would signal an early turn.
  4. China. From about 20% of revenue (2026) to about 15% (2027) without the MATCH Act; Q3 2026's 23% of system sales was a re-acceleration, not a new level. Observable: the year-end US defence bill (MATCH vehicle), the 10 November 2026 expiry of the Affiliates Rule suspension and China's rare-earth suspension, and the quarterly China share of system sales.

Scenarios to carry beside the base (my arithmetic).

  • Upside (about one in five): 2028 Low-NA 120, Bernstein-level pricing, IBM 16.5 billion EUR, GM 59.5%: FY2027 EPS about 61 EUR, FY2028 about 86 EUR.
  • MATCH Act enacted for 2027 (about one in four): Chinese immersion shipments and servicing to designated fabs stop; most freed immersion tools are re-allocated while capacity is short; China IBM falls about 0.8 billion EUR: FY2027 revenue about 53 billion and EPS about 52 EUR (-8%); FY2028 about 64.5 billion and 68 EUR (-9%).
  • AI-financing or memory shock in 2027 to 2028 (about one in five): 2028 Low-NA about 95, DUV -15%, upgrades down to about 3.5 billion EUR, GM 56%: FY2027 EPS about 53 EUR, FY2028 about 59 EUR (-22%).
  • 2029 digestion instead of a pause (about one in three for 2029): revenue -12%, GM 56%: FY2029 EPS about 61 EUR (-21% against my base) and FCF well below 0.7x net income.

Milestones for the next 18 months, with the reading my base implies.

  • 10 November 2026: US Affiliates Rule and China rare-earth suspensions expire unless extended.
  • December 2026: US defence bill (MATCH Act vehicle).
  • 27 January 2027: FY2026 out-turn (my base 44.6 billion EUR, GM 55.7 to 56%), FY2027 guide (my base is consistent with a range of about 52 to 58 billion and GM 56 to 58%), annual backlog (above about 45 billion EUR supports 2028), IBM guide (at least 13.5 billion EUR), Q1 2027 guide (about 12 to 13 billion EUR).
  • April 2027 (Q1 2027): IBM at least 3.3 billion EUR; China share of system sales at or below 20%; Q2 guide.
  • May 2027: delayering redundancies begin (small cost).
  • 10 June 2027 CMD: 2028 capacity above 110 Low-NA systems, a 2030 framework well above 60 billion EUR (my base 78.8 billion), a GM path to about 60%, and a new buyback programme.
  • July and October 2027 (Q2, Q3): first visible value-based pricing in the GM; NXE:3800F shipments; year-on-year revenue growth falling from about 44% (Q2) to about 20% (Q3) on base effects.
  • January 2028: FY2028 guide; whether 2029 orders are building (contract liabilities).

Where the first set's foundations show through. The monopoly and pricing evidence (first set C-41 to C-47) justifies holding EUV share at 100% and price rising through a downturn; the supply evidence (C-05, C-39) justifies the unit caps; the policy evidence (C-04, C-37) justifies the China path and the MATCH scenario; the base-rate evidence (C-06, C-84) justifies the FY2029 pause. The one first-set judgement I have changed is the cushion under FY2027 consensus (seasonality; see the reading notes).

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

A782nd of 49

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: there is substantial further scope, concentrated in three big areas (value-based pricing, EUV and new-model mix, and working capital) with several small ones behind them, all congruent with the stated strategy. The gross-margin scope is roughly 57 to 60%-plus by 2028 to 2030, and the cash scope is several billion EUR of working capital if tool cycle times fall.

  • Big area 1: pricing. Completed value-based resets (partial 2027, full 2028) on critical products, upgrades and services; Bernstein's EUV ASP +10% (2027) and +7% (2028); the 3800E to 3800F step (about +20% price over two years); parts pricing (Korea +10% from January 2027). Most should reach GM after ramp costs (CFO).
  • Big area 2: mix. EUV share of system sales rises on consensus from about 53% (FY2026: 17.3 of 32.5 billion EUR) to about 59% (FY2028: 32.3 of 54.6 billion EUR); High-NA moves toward corporate-level margins by the end of the decade as volume absorbs fixed costs; metrology (YieldStar 550) and software scale.
  • Big area 3: working capital. A cash-conversion cycle of about 300 days against a sub-200-day ambition; peers show what is possible (KLA and Tokyo Electron hold about 22 to 23 inventory days; ASM International runs working capital at about 6% of sales). Even a partial improvement would release several billion EUR and lift FCF conversion above 1.0x for a period.
  • Small areas. The delayering (about 200 million EUR a year), AI diagnostics, ocean freight, module-based shipping, supplier cost-downs.
  • Peer precedents. Morgan Stanley's bull case of 60% GM by 2028; Deutsche Bank's 60% by Q4 2028; Bernstein's above 60% GM and above 50% operating margin by 2030. These are achievable on the arithmetic of pricing plus mix if upgrade normalisation is gradual.
  • Congruence. Every lever sits inside the stated priorities (capacity, productivity, value pricing, holistic lithography, operational excellence).

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: the core growth engine has accelerated, not decelerated: trailing-twelve-month revenue growth is now about +22.5%, above the company's normal of about 18% a year, quarterly growth went from +0.7% to +55.2% in a year, and the input force (AI-driven lithography intensity) strengthened. The only signs of deceleration are second-order: EPS growth peaks in 2026, logic system growth is only about +10% in 2026, and the AI capex growth rate is forecast to slow in 2028.

  • Revenue: trailing twelve months to Q3 2026 about 39.5 billion EUR against about 32.2 billion EUR a year earlier; quarterly year-on-year growth +0.7%, +4.9%, +13.2%, +21.3%, +55.2%.
  • Input force: TSMC capex raised (2026 60 to 64 billion US dollars; 2027 about 75 to 80 billion expected); memory system sales +75% in 2026; DRAM EUV exposures forecast up 4.5 times over five years; orders for 2028 and requests for 2029 to 2030.

Credibility.

What each force contributes (net, FY2025 to FY2028).

  • Units (capacity). Low-NA 44 (FY2025) to 65, 84 and 110 systems; immersion about 130 to 160 to about 190; this is about two-thirds of revenue growth and is supply-paced, which is why it is predictable until 2028.
  • Price (P-04). About 3.8 billion EUR of FY2028 revenue at close to full margin; worth about +2.5 points of GM gross, about +1.2 points net of offsets.
  • Operating leverage (P-01). About +5.5 points of EBIT margin from FY2026 to FY2028, about half from operating expenses growing at half the rate of revenue.
  • Cost elimination (P-03). About zero net; the delayering saving is absorbed by hiring and the retention award.
  • Feedback loops (P-05). Positive on 2026 to 2027 margins (upgrades) and cash (down payments), turning negative on 2028 mix and 2029 cash.
  • Financial structure and below-EBIT items (P-02, P-08). Buybacks +5% to FY2028 EPS; tax and interest about -1%.

The shape of the next 18 months (the period set three must judge). Two features matter for predictability. First, Q1 2027 steps down about 15% from Q4 2026 on seasonality (Q1 fell 10 to 16% from Q4 in 2025 and 2026), which will look weak against a 14.7 billion EUR Q4 guide unless the market reads seasonality correctly. Second, reported growth decelerates to single digits by Q4 2027 on comparison base alone.

Width and skew of the cone. FY2027: narrow (EPS ±10%), mildly skewed up (upgrades, pricing) with a MATCH tail down. FY2028: wide (EPS -24% to +16% around my base), skewed down, because the downside combines volume, mix (upgrades) and policy while the upside is capped by supply. FY2029: very wide, skewed down.

Where I sit against consensus, and why it matters for set four. EBIT margins in line; revenue 2.6% (FY2027) and 5.5% (FY2028) below, mainly on China DUV and High-NA timing; EPS 2.5% and 2% below, because my larger buybacks offset part of the revenue gap; FY2029 far below thin consensus, because I carry a slowdown. The gap is small enough near term that the third and fourth sets should treat consensus FY2027 as broadly right and focus on the composition (IBM and China) rather than the level.

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is reflected in guidance least, in consensus mostly, and in the narrative almost entirely for the next 18 months and only partly beyond; the gap is favourable on guidance, neutral on consensus and modestly favourable on narrative, and its shape is the point. Guidance sits well below what we and the market expect (FY2026 at the 2030 framework's floor; FY2027 unguided; the framework itself a quarter below FY2028 consensus), and the company's habit of conservative opening ranges followed by staged raises is intact, so the direction of guidance over the window is up on substance; the cost is that the two opening guides in the window (January 2027 and January 2028) will each have a midpoint below consensus and will each give the market a chance to read a peak into a floor. Consensus is roughly right where we can see (FY2027 within 3%; FY2028 within 2%), will be held through the window with a small January revenue trim and a mid-year margin lift, and will be re-set for 2028 to 2030 by the June 2027 Capital Markets Day, which is the one event that can move estimates by more than 5% in either direction; the thin FY2029 consensus will fall toward our figure as coverage widens, and the memo should pre-empt the reading of that as a cut. The narrative knows the three numbers that decide 2028 and argues about them daily; it does not carry the sequence in which the case's loops turn, the cash-before-earnings shape of 2029, the small retention-award cost, the China servicing split, or the optics of reported growth falling to single digits by Q4 2027 on base effects.

The steer for the memo (topics #4, #7 and #8). Topic #4: guidance is conservative, management keeps expectations conservative by withholding the three numbers the market wants, and the record of raising guidance and estimates in an up-cycle is strong; the two guidance cuts in four years were at turning points, which the prepaid 2027 order book does not resemble. Topic #7: the scope for upgrades to guidance and estimates over six to twelve months is real but modest for FY2027 (+3 to +6% on EPS by October 2027) and large but binary for FY2028 to FY2030 at the CMD; the memo should state that the case does not need sell-side upgrades in the window to work, because the multiple already discounts a peak that the fundamentals have not reached. Topic #8: momentum is A on sell-side revisions and D on technicals and thematic reading, with the Buy share falling from a crowded level; the same split applies to peers (ASM International A and D; VAT A and D; BESI D and D), so ASML's momentum profile is the sector's, and the sector's regime (risk-off, Semiconductors Underweight, AI financing as the weak link) is the variable that decides whether the favourable guidance gap is paid for in the window or after it.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

A722nd of 49

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: over the six earnings events in the window (27 January 2027, April, July and October 2027, January 2028, April 2028) and the 10 June 2027 Capital Markets Day, guidance is more likely to be raised than cut on substance and more likely to look conservative than aggressive in form. My probabilities: the Q4 2026 guide is met or beaten at the January print (about 85%); the opening FY2027 range has a midpoint below consensus revenue of 57.5 billion EUR (about 60%), at it (25%) or above (15%); the FY2027 range is raised at least once during 2027 (about 70%), by a smaller amount than 2026's 22% because units are fixed (my central expectation is +3 to +6% at the midpoint, driven by IBM and price); a cut to FY2027 guidance inside the window has about a one-in-six probability, driven by a statutory China ban, a supply slip or a memory-led upgrade pause; the opening FY2028 guide (January 2028) has a midpoint below consensus (73.7 billion EUR) with about 55% probability unless the CMD has already set the 2028 unit number above 110. Secondarily: the 2030 framework (44 to 60 billion EUR; 56 to 60% GM; IBM 11 to 13 billion EUR) will be raised at the June 2027 CMD with near certainty (about 90%), because FY2026 already sits at its floor, FY2027 consensus near its ceiling, FY2028 consensus about 23% above its ceiling and IBM already above its 2030 target in 2026; the only question is by how much, and whether the GM ceiling moves to about 60 to 62%.

Event by event, with what drives each outcome.

  • 27 January 2027 (Q4 2026 and FY2027 guide). Q4 revenue guided 14.2 to 15.2 billion EUR; the record of beating the midpoint by 2 to 3% in four straight quarters and the Q3 beat on IBM make a print of 14.7 to 15.2 billion EUR likely (85%); GM about 57% guided, more likely above than below (upgrades beat guidance by 400 million EUR in Q3 and 262 million in Q2). The FY2027 range: ASML's pattern is an opening range whose midpoint sits well below its internal plan (January 2026: 34 to 39 billion EUR against an out-turn of 44 to 45), and management has given itself no reason to break it (capacity-ramp costs, High-NA mix and 3800F reliance were named as margin offsets in October; the CFO declined to say whether 2027 GM beats the Q4 exit rate). A range of about 52 to 58 billion EUR with GM 56 to 58% (the second set's expectation, which the T-I1 report's reading of the opening-guide habit supports) gives a midpoint about 4% below consensus revenue and a GM midpoint about a point below consensus (57.9%). The drivers of an above-consensus opening guide would be an IBM guide above 14.5 billion EUR and a backlog above about 55 billion EUR; the drivers of a below-consensus guide are the habit itself and the named margin offsets. Market reaction: the T-I1 report is right that a far-below-consensus opening guide would compress the multiple; the mitigants that would be in the same print are the backlog (first disclosure since 38.8 billion EUR), the Q1 guide (if above about 13 billion EUR) and the buyback.
  • April 2027 (Q1). Seasonal step-down (base about 12.6 billion EUR, -15%; the 2026 precedent was -10%): a beat on the base is more likely than a miss (60%); IBM at or above 3.3 billion EUR is the test. A raise to the FY2027 range at this print is unlikely (20%); a reaffirmation with a narrowed range likely.
  • July 2027 (Q2). The first quarter with visible value-based pricing in shipments and the first NXE:3800F deliveries; the most likely event for a raise (about 50% at this print), on GM and IBM rather than revenue, because units cannot move. The delayering's May 2027 severance (50 to 100 million EUR) is noise.
  • October 2027 (Q3). Reported growth decelerates to about +20% on the comparison base; a second raise or a narrowing to the top of the range (about 40%); the 2028 capacity number, if not given in June, must be given here.
  • January 2028 (Q4 2027 and FY2028 guide). FY2027 out-turn at or above the base (about 65%); the FY2028 opening range will be set on 2028 units and will again be conservative in form; if the CMD confirmed above 110 Low-NA units, a range of about 66 to 74 billion EUR is consistent with the base (69.7) and with a midpoint about 5% below consensus (73.7). A cut here (to the implied trajectory, not to a prior guide) would come from a 2028 order deferral visible in non-current CL during H2 2027.
  • April 2028 (Q1 2028). Outside my ability to call beyond the base; the first quarter that depends on 2028 output.

What would cut guidance, and what each requires. (i) A statutory ban on immersion shipments and servicing to designated Chinese fabs, implemented within the window: requires enactment in the December 2026 defence bill and an implementation timetable faster than Morgan Stanley's "a year or more"; the hit is 8 to 10% of a full year's EPS, larger in the first two quarters before re-allocation. (ii) A supply slip: requires a sub-tier failure (mechatronics, frames, valves) of the 2022 kind; the cost is deferred revenue and fast shipments, recovered within two quarters. (iii) An upgrade pause: requires customer utilisation to fall (a memory price break from mid-2027 is Jefferies' bear path); the 2023 precedent cut IBM growth from +5% to flat inside two quarters. (iv) Customer cleanroom delays: Morgan Stanley already records a DRAM customer moving tools from December 2026 to March 2027; this moves quarters, not years. None of these is the base; together they are the one-in-six.

What would raise guidance, and what each requires. (i) IBM running above 3.5 billion EUR a quarter through H1 2027 (requires cleanroom scarcity to persist, which it does through 2027 on every fab schedule in the sources). (ii) Pricing landing earlier or larger than "partial" (requires new-price orders shipping from mid-2027; Bernstein's +10% EUV ASP in 2027 is the upper bound). (iii) More 2027 immersion capacity than 170 (de-bottlenecking; Redburn's 35 to 40% system-sales growth case). (iv) Fewer China licence refusals than the base assumes (China at about 15% of revenue). The combination gives the +3 to +6% raise.

The secondary horizon (18 months to three years). The 2030 framework will be replaced at the CMD; the memo should expect revenue scenarios of roughly 65 to 90 billion EUR (the base's FY2030 is 78.8), a GM range reaching about 60% at its top (Deutsche Bank expects "line of sight to low-60s"; Bernstein above 60% and operating margin above 50% by 2030; the base is 58.8%), an IBM ambition above 20 billion EUR (Bernstein 23), a 2028 Low-NA capacity statement above 110, and a new or enlarged buyback. The probability that the new framework is set above what we model for 2030 is about 50%; the probability that it is set above consensus FY2029 (83.6 billion EUR on eight brokers) in its central scenario is lower, perhaps 35%, because management reiterates frameworks rather than stretching them (November 2024 held the 2030 range unchanged despite the October 2024 cut). A raised framework being met is then the 2029 question: on our base (a pause) a raised 2030 central scenario would be met only if 2029 is a pause rather than a decline (about 60%).

Why the gap is favourable. Current guidance (FY2026 at the 2030 floor; no FY2027 number; a framework the company's own FY2028 consensus exceeds by a quarter) sits far below our assessment, and the pattern of staged raises in up-cycles is intact. The cost is in form: the opening ranges will give the market two chances (January 2027 and January 2028) to read conservatism as a peak.

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

B5210th of 49

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: consensus FY2027 EPS (57.55 EUR median; 56.79 mean; +7% in one month, +37% in six, +79% in twelve) will most likely be held within a band of about 55 to 60 EUR through the window: a small cut to revenue after a conservative January guide (-2 to -4%), offset in EPS by IBM and gross margin, then upgrades of 3 to 6% after the July and October 2027 prints as pricing and upgrades show in the margin. Consensus FY2028 (76.44 EUR; revenue 73.7 billion EUR) is the line that will move, and the June 2027 CMD decides its direction: a 2028 unit number above 110 with a GM path toward 60% takes it to about 80 to 85 EUR (+5 to +10%); 110 units with GM flat takes it to about 70 to 72 (-6 to -8%). Our base (75.1) is 2% below consensus, so my central expectation for FY2028 consensus over 18 months is a drift down of a few per cent in H1 2027 (China, High-NA timing, the Q1 optics) and a recovery after the CMD, net roughly flat to +5%. The net breadth of revisions, now strongly positive (revision-momentum score 9; EPS up in each of the last one, three, six and twelve months), will narrow to mixed after January and turn positive again from July; the Buy share (76%, down four points in three months) is more likely to fall than rise, because the stock is a crowded consensus holding and the dissenters (Jefferies Hold at 1,590 EUR; AlphaValue Underweight at 1,472; a restricted Hold at 1,575) are arguing the cycle, not the company. Secondarily, consensus FY2029 (91.72 EUR on four brokers; revenue 83.6 billion EUR on eight) is too high against our 78 EUR and will come down as coverage widens, by perhaps 10%, which the market may read as a cut although much of it is a coverage artefact.

Where consensus sits against guidance and the run-rate, allowing for seasonality. FY2026 consensus (44.46 billion EUR) equals the guide's lower half and will be met. FY2027 consensus revenue (57.5 billion EUR) is 9% above H2 2026 annualised (about 52.9 billion EUR) and needs DUV systems growth of about 25% while China falls back, EUV growth of about 38% (units +30%, price and mix +8%) and IBM +19%; our base gets to 56.0 with IBM +16% and China at 15% of revenue. The brokers' FY2027 revenue range (Morgan Stanley 55.9; Fubon 56.6; Mizuho 56.9; Jefferies 58.1; Deutsche Bank 60.1; Evercore 61.1; Redburn 61.6) shows the median is not the high end: a January guide of 52 to 58 billion EUR would leave the lower half of the brokers untouched and trim the upper half. On EPS the range is tighter (52.3 to 62.2) because the high-revenue brokers carry more capacity cost and the low-revenue brokers more margin. The FactSet median (57.55) appears to include the Mistral-free basis for 2027 either way; the FY2026 median (42.49) includes the Mistral gain and the mean (41.19) does not, which the Consumer must keep straight when the January print is compared with "consensus".

The path, event by event.

  • November to January: peers' results (Lam's calendar 2027 WFE above 200 billion US dollars; Tokyo Electron above 190; ASM's 2027 guide above 5.7 billion EUR; VAT's record orders) keep the sector's revision breadth positive; ASML's FY2027 EPS drifts +1 to +2% on IBM. The two policy dates can cut it by 3 to 8% if a ban is enacted with a fast timetable (about one in six).
  • 27 January 2027: the opening guide trims FY2027 revenue consensus by 2 to 4% (to about 55.5 to 56.5 billion EUR) with EPS roughly held (the IBM guide and a GM range of 56 to 58% offset); the annual backlog (above about 50 billion EUR) and the Q1 guide decide whether FY2028 consensus holds or slips. Breadth turns mixed: the revision count will show cuts to revenue and holds on EPS, which the dashboard will score as a fall in momentum.
  • April 2027: a Q1 beat on the base restores EPS drift; FY2028 untouched.
  • 10 June 2027 (CMD): the single largest revision event in the window for FY2028 and the only one for FY2029 to FY2030. Above 110 units, a 60% GM path and a larger buyback: FY2028 +5 to +10%, FY2029 to FY2030 estimates established for the first time by a wide broker set at levels near our 2030 (78.8 billion EUR revenue) rather than the thin consensus's. At 110 units and a GM framework ending at 60% only in 2030: FY2028 -5 to -8%.
  • July and October 2027: pricing in the GM, upgrades still strong: FY2027 EPS +3 to +6% cumulatively; FY2028 follows the CMD's direction. The deceleration in reported growth (+20% in Q3, +8% in Q4) will not cut estimates (estimates are annual) but will test breadth.
  • January 2028: the FY2028 opening guide (midpoint likely below the then-consensus) repeats the January 2027 pattern one year on; FY2029 consensus starts its descent from 91.72 as more brokers publish a 2029 and the 2029 digestion debate opens.

Why consensus will be held rather than cut on FY2027 even if the guide disappoints. Three reasons. Revisions at ASML follow guidance with a lag and have been too low throughout this up-cycle (FY2026 EPS consensus +59% in twelve months; FY2027 +79%; FY2028 +106%): brokers now know the opening guide is a floor. The variance in FY2027 sits in IBM and margin, where beats have been consistent for six quarters. And the backlog disclosure in the same print converts the inferred (56 billion EUR) into the official, which protects the FY2028 line even if the FY2027 midpoint is soft. The historical reversal precedents (2022: recession-driven push-outs; 2024: logic yield delays and DUV digestion) required a demand event that the prepaid 2027 order book does not have.

Breadth and positioning. The net breadth of EPS revisions for FY2027 is positive in every window in the FactSet data; the target-price revision (+42% in six months; +1% in one) has stalled, which is the first sign that brokers are no longer raising fair values with estimates; the Buy share has fallen four points. The T-I2 report's finding that the market is crowded (76% Buy) and that disappointment "would trigger sharp, non-linear multiple de-ratings" is the risk to breadth, not to level. My expectation: the Buy share drifts to about 70% over the window as a few houses move to Hold on valuation discipline after a January guide below consensus; the EPS level holds.

Secondary horizon. FY2029 EPS consensus (91.72, four brokers) implies +20% growth on FY2028 and revenue 39% above the old 2030 ceiling; our base has +4%. As coverage widens after the CMD the median will fall toward 80 to 85 EUR even if the bulls (Bernstein 84 for 2028; Redburn about 91) hold their views, because the entrants will model a slower 2029. The FY2029 gross-profit line (identical to FY2028 at 42,408) and the 54% EBITDA margin are artefacts and will disappear with coverage. The memo should present the FY2029 "cut" that is coming as mostly arithmetic and partly the genuine digestion debate.

Why the gap is only neutral. Our FY2027 is 3% below consensus EPS and our FY2028 2% below; we are not calling for upgrades that the sell-side has missed, and revision momentum is already the strongest in the European group (A on the dashboard). The favourable elements are the opening-guide floor, the margin beats and the CMD's potential to establish 2029 to 2030 numbers; the unfavourable elements are the January revenue trim, the FY2029 descent and the crowding. Net, consensus is roughly right near term, and the memo should not build its case on sell-side upgrades in the window; it should build it on the June reset of the long-range numbers.
