Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 27 October 2026, 23:08 to 23:22 UK, Fable, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
ASML (ASML-NL): answers to the third set (predictability of the 18-month forecasts) and the fourth set (guidance, consensus and narrative)

Written 2026-10-27 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo for Richard.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer

Conventions. Money in EUR millions unless stated; per-share figures in EUR. GM is gross margin; IBM is Installed Base Management (service, field upgrades, productivity software); CL is contract liabilities (customer down payments); Low-NA is the NXE EUV platform, High-NA the EXE platform. "The 18-month window" is the six reporting periods from Q4 2026 to Q1 2028, which is also the model's 18-month column (ending 31 March 2028). "The base" is the Financial Analyst's model after update 1: revenue 44,558 / 56,010 / 69,676 / 71,627 / 78,790 for FY2026 to FY2030; EPS 41.92 / 55.94 / 75.11 / 78.12 / 91.67 (Viewforth definition, undiluted); against FactSet consensus medians of 44,464 / 57,507 / 73,683 / 83,599 and 42.49 / 57.55 / 76.44 / 91.72. The model's EPS sits within 2% of the second set's cross-check in every year, so the two can be read interchangeably; where I quote a single FY2027 base EPS I use 56 EUR.

The cross-check gaps in free cash flow, explained (the instruction asks for this; the change to the input is in section 6 of the reading notes and in inputs-changed.md). The one-page summary flags every forecast year: the second set's FCF against the model's is 15,020 against 8,594 (FY2026), 20,300 against 15,444 (FY2027), 23,580 against 21,242 (FY2028), 19,870 against 23,529 (FY2029) and 32,900 against 29,822 (FY2030). The gaps are +6,426, +4,856, +2,338, -3,659 and +3,078. They are not forecasting disagreements; they are one missing line. The model's working-capital input is defined on the financials block's basis (receivables plus inventories less payables), which excludes contract liabilities, and the model has no separate line for them. The second set's path for year-end CL (18.9, 25.5, 30.0, 32.0, 28.0, 31.0 billion EUR) implies cash inflows of +6,600, +4,500, +2,000, -4,000 and +3,000. Subtracting those from the five gaps leaves residuals of -174, +356, +338, +341 and +78, which are the roughly 300 million EUR a year of non-cash share-award expense (2027 to 2029) that the second set added back and the model evidently does not, and in FY2026 the 510 million EUR non-cash Mistral gain the second set deducted. So: gap = contract-liability flow + share-based pay add-back, to within 1 to 2% of each year's FCF. The model's FY2026 figure (8,594) is also wrong on the evidence, not merely on definition: nine-month FCF was already about 10,100 (Q1 -2,608, Q2 +1,317, Q3 +11,351), so the model implies a negative Q4, in the quarter in which ASML collects most of its receivables; the second set's 15,020 implies a Q4 of about 4,900, which is consistent with the seasonal pattern. Judgement: the second set's figures are the right economics; the model's understate FCF by about 6.4 and 4.9 billion EUR in FY2026 and FY2027, overstate it by 3.7 billion EUR in FY2029, and therefore show net cash falling to 8.8 billion EUR by FY2028 when it should rise toward about 19 to 21 billion EUR. This matters for the memo's balance-sheet and capital-return narrative (buybacks of 13 and 17 billion EUR look unaffordable in the model and are comfortably affordable in reality), and for the cash-turns-before-earnings signal in 2029 that the second set rightly emphasised. The input I change is the working-capital line, re-expressed to carry the down-payment flow (section 6 below).

Five things to carry into the memo from these two sets.

  1. The 18-month forecasts are among the most predictable in the European universe on revenue (units capped and ordered; 2027 Low-NA fixed at about 85 by long-lead parts) and ordinarily predictable on margin; the cone for FY2027 EPS is about 50 to 61 EUR around a base of 56 (consensus 57.55), roughly symmetrical in its centre with a fatter left tail from policy. The window's last column (Q1 2028) is the first that depends on 2028 units.
  2. The drivers are concentrated, not diffuse: three variables (Low-NA units, the size of value-based pricing, the path of IBM upgrades) explain about two-thirds of the revenue step from FY2025 to FY2028 and almost all of the margin step; China policy is the fourth and the swing. Concentration is why 2027 is predictable (two of the three are contracted) and why 2028 is not (two of the three are not).
  3. Guidance will be raised in substance and will look conservative in form: the 27 January 2027 FY2027 range is more likely than not to have a midpoint below consensus revenue (57.5 billion EUR), the 10 June 2027 Capital Markets Day is near-certain to replace the 2030 framework with one far above it, and the risk of a cut within 18 months is real but low (about one in six), concentrated in policy and supply.
  4. Consensus FY2027 is roughly right and will be held; consensus FY2028 is the battleground and will move with the June 2027 unit number; consensus FY2029 (four brokers, 91.72 EUR) is too high against our 78 EUR and will drift down as coverage widens, which the market may read as a cut.
  5. The narrative already carries most of our dynamics (2028 units, pricing, upgrade cyclicality, MATCH, High-NA timing, the de-rating playbook); what it does not carry is the sequence in which the loops turn (upgrades, then down payments, then units), the retention-award expense, the optics of reported growth falling from about +50% to single digits by Q4 2027 on base effects, and the probability that 2029 is a decline rather than a pause. The share price is about 62% sector-driven on a rolling six months, so the idiosyncratic third is what January and June can move.

Where I disagree with the earlier sets, and why.

  • The second set's correction of the first set's run-rate cushion stands. The first set (C-68, C-49, judgement 1 of its summary) said FY2027 consensus was undemanding because it sat below the Q4 2026 run-rate annualised; the second set showed Q4 is seasonally about a third of the year. I side with the second set and add the arithmetic: H2 2026 annualised (about 52.9 billion EUR) plus 30% more Low-NA units on a 2026 EUV base of about 16.9 billion EUR, plus price and IBM growth, lands at 56 to 58 billion EUR. Consensus is achievable, not slack.
  • The second set's MATCH sizing (about 8 to 10% of FY2027 EPS) is right in magnitude but its probability is misplaced in time. The AlphaSense evidence (own-2) shows re-allocation of banned immersion tools takes seven to twelve months because tools are customer-configured and the receiving cleanrooms must be ready, which argues for a harsher first-year hit; but Morgan Stanley (28 September 2026) says meaningful controls would take a year or more to implement after enactment, which argues that a December 2026 enactment lands on 2028, not 2027. The two offset. I would put the probability of a MATCH-driven P&L effect inside the 18-month window at about one in six (the second set used one in four for 2027), and about one in three for a 2028 effect.
  • The second set's probability of a 2029 decline (one in three) is a little low. Every ASML growth run has ended in digestion, the AI-financing loop is now visibly stressed, and consensus FY2029 rests on four brokers. I would use about 40%, which does not change the base (a pause) but raises the weight the memo should give the downside scenario for the three-year horizon.
  • The first set's quarterly seasonal input is at the severe end. The second set's Q1 2027 revenue (about 12.6 billion EUR, -15% from Q4 2026) uses the 2025 precedent (-16%); the 2026 precedent was -10% (guided -11%). The capacity ramp, which raises Q1 shipments against a Q4 that is itself capacity-limited, argues for a smaller drop (about -12%). I leave the input, because a conservative Q1 is the right lean against the January guide, but the Consumer should treat the Q1 2027 print as more likely to beat the base than miss it.
  • The model's working-capital input must change (above and section 6). This is the only forecast input I change.

Section 6 (changes to the forecast inputs), in one paragraph. I change "Working capital (% of revenue)" from 41.5 / 41.0 / 40.0 / 43.0 / 40.0 to 26.7 / 21.2 / 21.2 / 30.3 / 24.6 for FY2026 to FY2030. The new figures are the trade working capital the model already carries, less the second set's year-by-year change in contract liabilities, rebased so that the FY2025 opening balance stays at the FactSet actual (11,695, 35.8% of revenue) and no double-count of the 18.9 billion EUR of down payments already on the balance sheet occurs. With them the model's FCF reproduces the second set's within about 300 million EUR a year (the share-award add-back), and net cash rises through FY2028 instead of falling. The proper fix is a contract-liability line in the model; until it exists this re-expression is the only way to make the forecasts of record carry the cash that ASML's customers pay in advance. The reason sentence in inputs-changed.md says so.

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: Risks · Group: Guidance · Group: Sell-side estimates · Group: Stock market narrative

Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Changes to the forecast inputs: ASML Holding NV (ASML-NL), from the third and fourth sets, 2026-10-27

Money in millions of EUR, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the lines changed are shown, in the form of Parts 1 to 3 of the second set's forecast inputs; every other line is unchanged.

Part 2. Cash flow and balance sheet inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Working capital (% of revenue) 26.7 21.2 21.2 30.3 24.6

The model has no contract-liability line, so its free cash flow omitted the customer down payments (year-end contract liabilities of about 18.9, 25.5, 30.0, 32.0, 28.0 and 31.0 billion EUR from FY2025 to FY2030, cash flows of +6,600, +4,500, +2,000, -4,000 and +3,000) that explain the whole of the flagged gap between the second set's free cash flow and the model's (15,020 against 8,594 in FY2026; 20,300 against 15,444; 23,580 against 21,242; 19,870 against 23,529; 32,900 against 29,822, with residuals of under 400 a year that are the non-cash share-award add-back); these new percentages are the second set's trade working capital (41.5, 41.0, 40.0, 43.0 and 40.0% of revenue) less the year-by-year change in contract liabilities, rebased so that the FY2025 opening balance stays at the FactSet actual of 11,695 (35.8% of revenue) and the 18.9 billion EUR of down payments already on the balance sheet is not counted twice, so that the model's free cash flow reproduces the second set's within about 300 million EUR a year and its year-end net cash rises through FY2028 (to about 19 to 21 billion EUR) instead of falling to 8.8 billion EUR; the proper fix is a contract-liability line in the model, and this re-expression should be replaced by one when it exists.

Working capital is the balance at the end of each fiscal year as a percentage of the last twelve months' revenue; on this re-expressed basis it is trade working capital net of the cumulative change in contract liabilities since FY2025.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of ASML-NL with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-26 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: ASML Holding N.V. (ASML-NL; ASML), the Dutch sole supplier of EUV lithography systems and leading supplier of DUV immersion and dry lithography, metrology and inspection tools; it reports two lines, Net System Sales (EUV Low-NA and High-NA, DUV, metrology and inspection) and Installed Base Management (service, field upgrades and productivity software for the installed fleet).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For ASML Holding N.V. (ASML-NL), what are investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, saying and doing about the company: in buy-side and hedge-fund commentary, investor letters, conference and podcast discussion, and social media including Twitter, since the Q3 2026 results of 14 October 2026, on the 2028 Low-NA EUV capacity number (management said on 14 October 2026 that 30% growth in 2027 and 2028 "will not meet customer demand" and is investigating more for 2028; Morgan Stanley reported on 15 October 2026 that some investors hoped for 120 to 125 systems), the completed value-based pricing negotiations, the cyclicality of productivity-upgrade revenue inside Installed Base Management, the proposed US MATCH Act in the year-end defence bill, the financing of AI data-centre capital spending, and whether 2028 is the cycle's peak; and what positioning data show (fund ownership changes, short interest, options skew and implied volatility around the 27 January 2027 results, ETF and index flows), with 76% of covering brokers rating the stock Buy on 25 October 2026 and the shares about 19% below their 52-week high; for each theme, who says it, how prominently, since when, and with what view, separating the next 18 months from 18 months to three years out.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For ASML Holding N.V. (ASML-NL), how did its forward price-to-earnings multiple and its share price behave in the six months before and the twelve months after each of its past Capital Markets Days and long-range framework updates (November 2022, November 2024) and each of its opening annual guidance statements in January (2022 to 2026), against the sell-side consensus at the time; how did the multiple behave in the two previous supply-constrained up-cycles (2017 to 2018, 2021 to 2022) between the point at which consensus earnings for the second forward year had risen by more than 50% in twelve months and the point at which the revenue growth rate peaked; and how has ASML's premium or discount to Applied Materials, Lam Research, KLA, Tokyo Electron, ASM International and BE Semiconductor on a two-year-forward price-to-earnings basis moved through those cycles and where does it stand today (ASML's twenty-four-month-forward multiple was about 19.7x on 23 October 2026 against a five-year range of about 18.1x to 40.4x); with dated evidence on what the multiple did when the company's reported year-on-year quarterly growth decelerated sharply because of comparison effects rather than falling orders.

Request: OWN-3

Question: For ASML Holding N.V. (ASML-NL), what do sell-side analysts and the company's own statements indicate the 27 January 2027 fourth-quarter 2026 results will contain, line by line: the expected range for the FY2027 revenue and gross-margin guidance against the FactSet median FY2027 revenue of 57,507 million EUR and EPS of 57.55 EUR (25 October 2026), the first-quarter 2027 revenue guide against the Q4 2026 guide of 14.2 to 15.2 billion EUR, the FY2027 Installed Base Management guide after Q4 2026 guidance of about 3.7 billion EUR, the annual backlog (last disclosed at 38.8 billion EUR at end-2025; Bernstein inferred about 56 billion EUR on 19 October 2026), any 2028 Low-NA EUV capacity number, 2027 capital expenditure, the share buyback (12 billion EUR programme for 2026 to 2028, about 6.1 billion EUR executed by 30 September 2026) and the dividend; how the share price reacted on the day of and the month after each January results release from 2022 to 2026 when the opening guide was above, at or below consensus; and what Applied Materials, Lam Research, KLA, Tokyo Electron, TSMC, Samsung, SK hynix and Micron have guided or are expected to guide for calendar 2027 capital spending and wafer-fab-equipment demand in their results between November 2026 and January 2027.

Terms used in the answers

Hover over a dotted term on a computer to see it written out. On a phone, this list is the reference. Terms not listed are company, product or trial names, or terms the Investment Analyst explains where it uses them.

EUR

FCF

FY

H1

H2
